Implementation: the PDP digests the request on receipt and its emitter signs no record without one of the two digests; a PEP refusal records the resource it established and digests the request or the pre-request input (review P2 fix: a resolved resource is kept). Ledger: the Decision Evidence digest row is tested; the Refusal Record digest row stays partial because the reference PEP's refusals never carry `parameter_digest`; the batch row stays todo (no batch endpoint). |

The demo templates carry a 30-day cadence. Ledger: `template.consent.review-cadence-stops-dispatch` and `template.members.review-cadence-shape` tested. |

| D231 | Model-judge adjudication merged (2026-10-02): #1012 (2bb99535), #1011 (e32652ff); #1010 open for the generative-derivation follow-up | On the owner's go ("merge"). The OAuth binding's `adjudication` `kind: policy` is the single home of the model-assisted approval rule: the policy decides deterministically over recorded inputs; a model's output can be one recorded input, with the model's identifier and version, that refuses or narrows, and the AS MUST NOT let it supply or widen authority; a model is never itself the deciding policy or workflow. The prose rule's MUST NOT gate issuance now names adjudicating policies and model inputs; the human Approver's prose check is unchanged. The architecture's Approve and Record names the three approval roles and summarizes the rule, and Shaping points to it. The Approval Governance Record's accountable assertion is `human` or `policy`, never `service`, and `authority` is defined for a `service` assertion (logic and model version). Review fixes before merge: the architecture's prose sentence scoped to the policy and its model inputs, Shaping's restriction attached to the model's output, and a `core.governance-record` facet row. Ledger: five `todo` rows (three core, including the facet; two approval-governance); core re-pinned to 4aa38402, approval-governance to 367e69e4. The wiki FAQ entry follows #1010 Sketch step 6, with the corrected profile-specific human-decision classes. |
| D232 | Capacity across Missions merged (2026-10-02): #992 (9324b094); #816 stays open | Owner go ("apply fixes", then "merge") on the analysis of Patrick Parker's "Laws of AI Effect Authority": a consumption bound limits the delegation it is approved for, not a credential or record derived from it. Metering gains Capacity Across Missions. A Child Mission's action is charged to its own bound and every ancestor's. Across consistency domains the child's bound is instead an escrowed allocation, no larger than the ancestor's remaining quantity, whose remainder returns only after the child's allocation is closed to new reservations. A Mission Issuer that can do neither refuses child creation. An Expansion successor or carryover replacement continues its predecessor's counters. A policy successor MUST NOT raise a bound; omitting a bound, a `max_calls` class or a `max_egress_volume` dimension counts as raising it. A raised bound renders consumption to date. A template consent presents the bound per instance with `max_active` and `dispatch_rate`. Settlement: lease expiry never releases an unsettled reservation in any action class (the idempotent/reversible exception from f494e556 is removed). A metered high-consequence idempotency horizon reaches the Mission's `expires_at`. Template and Progressive keep #660's decision (bound off the prohibited lists) with a corrected rationale. Runtime adds an informative consideration that an idempotency key names a request, not a business event; consistent with D223. Metering carries no ledger rows; they land with #816, whose Acceptance gains the two-children and fresh-credential cases. Template, Progressive and Runtime re-pinned; two review findings (escrow fence, bound omission) fixed before merge. |
| D233 | Critique follow-ons merged (2026-10-02): #996 (6f435daa), #995 (c5764e89), #1005 (f9aaf65d), #1006 (a89399cd), #1007 (65950150); #986 and #987 closed, #985 stays open | The five D219 PRs, merged on the owner's word after an owner review (five P2 findings, all fixed before merge) and branch updates with main (#978, #980, #992, #1012). #986: #995 groups the Operation Profile as binding and declarations with no normative change (keyword sequence identical); #1007 makes the Materialized Policy View one realization of the authority input, adds the evaluated-representation bound, scopes AuthZEN's PDP-side consistency checks to the loaded view or the directly evaluated Mission record, makes Runtime Evidence's `mission.policy_view_id` REQUIRED only where a view is used (`authority_hash` and the new `pdp_policy_version` otherwise), and moves the three policy-view rows to `runtime.materialized-policy-view` outside both baselines. #987: #996 states the per-Mission bound model in the architecture's Composition note; #1005 corrects the bound arithmetic (12 concurrently non-terminal descendants; 120 is not a lifetime ceiling) in the core, Derivation Limits, and the security model, and Child Delegation renders the subtree figures distinctly at their effective values, for policy-created children too, stating whether a finite aggregate is enforced. Owner ruling on #987 open question 1, by merge: Derivation Limits states that a child-creation token exchange is not counted against the parent's limit and the child's redemption of its initial grant counts against the child's own. #985 (specification half): #1006 defines the activation policy reference `{id, version, digest}` with the `mission-activation-policy` digest in the core; Template (`dispatch_policy`, new step 3, new `dispatch_policy_changed` reason, digest on the `template` claim member), Progressive (`drawdown_policy` object; cadence anchored with the committed policy), and Child Delegation (`child_creation_policy`) point to it; those shape choices are ruled by merge. Remaining on #985: the reference implementation (digest verification on the three activation paths, the `child_creation_policy` type change, fixtures, recomputed ceiling-hash vectors). |
| D234 | Agent selection merged (2026-10-02): #982 (c5a47bd0); #970 left open for the owner | Implements the D201 and D205 rulings. The Mission Issuer selects a dispatched instance's Agent from `allowed_recipients` `agents` under the Dispatch Policy, never from Dispatcher input: one listed Agent directly, several through the Dispatch Policy's selection rule, never by array order; the rule is part of the digest-committed Dispatch Policy (#1006 era `dispatch_policy` reference). The selection is committed as the instance `client_id`, kept on retries, and separate from Dispatcher authentication and the token's presenter binding. Implementation: `selectDispatchAgent` and `buildAuthorizationServer({ dispatchPolicies })`; `DispatchInput.recipient` removed; an unresolvable selection is refused with the implementation-local `agent_not_selected` on `access_denied`. Ledger: `recipient-agent-membership` partial to tested; four new rows tested. |
| D235 | AAuth transaction authorization matrix reclassified (2026-10-02): #1009 (20eccee7); #837 closed | Owner ruling on #837: the recommended reclassification at -11 and R3 -00, merged in #1009. The informative matrix in `draft-mcguinness-mission-aauth` classifies each Carrier Binding Floor slot as supplied, supplied conditionally, unproven equivalence, or absent, and names the signed native field and verifier step. Challenge carrier: supplied. Possession: supplied on both the `401` retry and the `202` poll (`Signature-Key` covered, `cnf.jwk` equals the signing key). Workflow handle: supplied for `202` only. Gaps: the pending-workflow lifetime, the `401` handle, and admission idempotency. Result class absent, because `r3_granted` is required on every R3 token. At most one result: per token only, and no issuance guard, since issuers keep no replay state for resource tokens (-11 6.7.1, 11.3.4.2). Operation supersession absent; current state conditional; parameter commitment unproven equivalence. A paragraph explains why R3's raw proposal commitment is not shown equivalent to the normalized `parameter_digest`. Release-of-result approval (R3 10.4) is treated separately from execution. The capability non-claim is unchanged. Table cells cite bare section numbers so the table fits 72 columns. Adds the informative runtime reference and its manifest edge. Three review findings at `ee574c10` (pending lifetime, `r3_granted`, duplicate issuance) were fixed before merge. #445 A15-A18 were refreshed; A18's verification is resolved, leaving only a cross-reference to ask for. Remaining AAuth: #834 half 1, #838, #836, and the decide items #839, #965, #966, #967, #991. |
| D236 | Owner ruling on #999 (2026-10-02): policy cannot restore contained capability, across a chain; PR #1025 opened | (1) A policy-adjudicated successor whose authority overlaps contained capability requires fresh human approval disclosing the capability and its event class. (2) The trigger is authority overlap with the complete successor Authority Set, including carried-forward authority; unrelated in-ceiling drawdowns stay policy-eligible under the existing guards. (3) An intervening successor cannot erase the restriction: the issuer retains containment history across the chain, and only a fresh human approval explicitly restoring the affected authority with its cause disclosed clears it; an unrelated approval or ceiling renewal does not. (4) The successor's overlay stays initially empty; the retained history governs future policy-drawdown eligibility only. #1025 defines the chain-retained containment restriction in Containment (it also closes the human-expansion twin, where an unrelated approved successor hid X's cause from a later restoring expansion), adds Progressive's overlap fallback, chain-wide test, ceiling-review disclosure item and Conformance bullet, three `todo` rows under `progressive.ceiling-drawdown`, and re-pins Progressive. Preserves D70; #998 undecided; the discharge guard's predecessor-only shape is a candidate follow-up. |
| D237 | #829 issuer-qualified source gates merged (2026-10-02): #1024 (5961830d); #829 closed; #827 unblocked | The source gates compared bare sub, so a foreign-issuer approver matched a local activator. The kernel binds its authority-source catalog to its principal namespace at construction (KernelOptions.principalIssuer, default the kernel issuer; configuration only; a catalog bound to another issuer refuses construction). assertLocalPrincipal requires non-empty string iss and sub with iss byte-equal to the namespace, no normalization; assertApproverMayActivate and assertSubjectDiscipline take the bound catalog and run it first, before the user_delegated return. kernel.approve guards both principals before derivation; template consent and dispatch run a namespace-only check before their idempotent returns (review of #829), so retries after revocation still return the original. Fixtures aligned: cross-org origin kernels configure the org identity-provider namespace; a discharge case that approved another AS's principals now uses its own. Eleven witnesses, six mutations each caught; citations added to core.approval-event.authority-source-activation and core.authority-sources.subject-discipline (both tested). Provider port doc states the canonical-local-principal contract. |
| D238 | Containment fallback merged (2026-10-02): #1025 (6144f299); #999 closed | Implements D236. Containment's restoration rule gains a chain-retained containment restriction: retained across every later successor, surfaced in any later expansion consent whose authority overlaps it, and cleared only by a fresh human approval explicitly restoring the affected authority with its cause disclosed. Progressive gains the overlap fallback over the complete successor Authority Set, the chain-wide test that neither an unrelated approval nor a ceiling review clears, a ceiling-review disclosure item, and a Conformance bullet. | D239 | D238 follow-ups filed (2026-10-02): #1026, #1027; #998 kept open | Owner direction after #999 closed. #1026 (decide, P2, bug): Progressive's discharge guard tests only the immediate predecessor, so discharging X in A, creating unrelated successor B, then requesting X from B restores X by policy; asks for a chain-wide discharge restriction consistent with D238 and whether a human restoration discloses the discharge. #1027 (decide, bug): Metering's exclusivity latch is per Mission, and nothing carries a group or its latch state to a child or successor; filed at P2 rather than P3 under the owner's rule, because policy-approved child creation and policy drawdown bypass a "never both" exclusion rendered to the Approver as enforced; drops to P3 if the ruling is per Mission by design. #302: #1026 takes #999's rank 45, #1027 is rank 54, P3 ranks shift by one. #998 stays open for its own completion-transport ruling; D238 does not decide it. |
| D240 | Owner ruling on #1020 to #1023 (2026-10-02): whitepaper-review dispositions; #1020 and #1021 to `now` | Owner review of the four issues filed from the review of the drafts against the Mission-Bound Authorization whitepaper (checked against main 20eccee7), adopted as the ruling. #1020: option B. A Runtime MUST over acting credentials used for high-consequence actions, whether the Mission is credential-carried or externally established; the enforcing component verifies the binding and a current proof of possession, not a `cnf` member alone. The DPoP and mTLS realization lives in Runtime OAuth. The OAuth binding stays at RFC 9700 Section 2.2.1's level, and Cross-Domain's attribution of a RECOMMENDED level to the binding is corrected. Refresh-token requirements are unchanged. The guarantee is stated as theft of the token alone; compromise of the key or its signing interface remains (RFC 9700 Section 4.10.1). #1021: cross-issuer joint authorization is outside the family's scope. Evidence correlation stays a possible future investigation and is never described as joint approval, which would also need required-approval, validity, withdrawal and action-boundary rules. #1022: proceeds with the narrowed sentence: revocation prevents further issuance and drawdown under the Mission, blocks pending Expansion completion, and cascades to children as Child Delegation specifies; outstanding credentials stay within the revocation window; equivalent authority under a separately approved Mission is not prohibited. Preventing re-approval needs policy over the relevant approval paths; revoking one template or client blocks only that route. For-cause disclosure is a separate follow-on to #705. #1023: stays parked. When triggered, cardinality, mutability, lifetime and revocation relationships come before identifiers or token members; #705 informs the mapping without blocking it; no correlation member is justified yet. Issue bodies carry Decisions of record; #302 tiers updated. |
| D241 | Owner ruling on #1026 and #1000 (2026-10-02), combined; PR #1031 opened | #1026, option (a): a committed discharge creates a restriction retained across the expansion chain; every successor's complete Authority Set, carried-forward authority included, is tested by authority overlap, not entry identity; an overlapping policy drawdown falls back to fresh human approval; unrelated drawdowns stay eligible; restoration needs an approval explicitly authorizing the affected authority with the discharged entry and its condition disclosed; an intervening successor, unrelated approval or routine ceiling renewal does not clear it; partial restoration clears only the restored authority; the discharge latch never reverts. Placement: retention in Discharge, guard and review disclosure in Progressive, human-restoration disclosure in Expansion. #1000: one Effective Authority Set and one mandatory gate; a Runtime-governed decision MUST refuse discharged authority, with discharge-aware state within the freshness bound and fail-closed otherwise (lifecycle-only `active` insufficient); issuance-only Discharge stays valid but does not meet Runtime's current-effective-authority guarantee at a boundary that omits discharge. **Supersedes D93's preservation of SHOULD-level runtime refusal.** Containment's Status and introspection authority projections MUST omit contained capability at action grain (no annotation format); history retained for audit and restoration disclosure. #998's transport and #896's shared condition facts stay undecided. |
| D242 | OAuth binding: #1019 Sketch step 1 clarifications merged (PR #1029, f236601e) | Owner's go 2026-10-02 to open #1019 Sketch step 1 as one core PR (resolves its open question 7), then to merge after one review round (two P2 findings fixed: refresh routing bounded by both the presented refresh grant and the Authority Set; a delegate is a distinct actor holding a delegated token, with the approved client's self-exchange preserved). Remediation Grains routes remediated entries by containment; a Non-Goal for joint outcomes and cross-resource effects (the `mission` claim does not identify a business transaction); intermediaries placed in Delegation; RFC 9728 `authorization_details_types_supported` cited. Beside settlement, the PDP makes one read-only query of the PEP: the consumption status of a stored `evaluation_id` from its D28 redemption store, asked only for a retransmission candidate and never cached; anything but `unconsumed` suppresses. Human release is an explicit, scoped relaxation by an Approver authorized for the originating restriction, disclosing the group and side, execution history and unresolved permits, the authority enabled and the affected branches; the historical latch is retained, and an ordinary child approval, unrelated approval, ceiling renewal or partial approval relaxes nothing it does not name. | D268 | #939 ruled (2026-10-03): optional request-provenance evidence profile; tier `decide` to `now` | On the owner's instruction to rule #939. Approve `draft-mcguinness-oauth-mission-request-provenance`, Standards-Track category with experimental spec maturity, as a separate optional Submission Evidence type (`mission-request-provenance`, signed JWT with protected `mission-request-provenance+jwt`, ES256 mandatory to implement). Trusted intake or a verified user-device key attests captured request origin within its configured authority; the shaper remains untrusted. Capture precedes shaping; signing afterward binds the retained request to one AS, exact Intent and presenter, without certifying faithful interpretation or approval. Choose domain-separated HMAC-SHA-256 with a fresh random 256-bit secret per capture, retained at trusted intake and omitted from the assertion and recorded facts; public salted hashes do not meet the guessing-resistance claim. The AS verifies the authorized signature, not an undisclosed input's HMAC; optional Shaping Evidence links remain audit commitments, with no leaked unsalted input digest. Require bounded validity, atomic replay handling, framework refusal and completed-creation recovery semantics, role-correct vectors and privacy limits. New Draft Admission is answered in the issue: existing admission assertions lack this request-capture contract; keep the framework type-free and Shaping Informational, without a normative Experimental Envelope dependency. The PS MUST authorize and record the action as the authenticated Person and MUST NOT apply a management service's administrative privilege. An internal invocation is not a control-plane request but keeps the same authorization, atomic transition and logging duties. A management service acting for a human is authorized and recorded as that human, with the service as the channel; how the human is conveyed is a deployment trust relationship, used only for a service registered for it. A browser session never authenticates a control-plane request. An informative trace and a Person Interfaces security subsection were added (cross-site submission, account or tenant switching, stale sessions, confused deputy). No user login is defined. Status keeps its SHOULD for distinct/elevated resume authorization; this ruling requires the same resume authorization the deployment applies to a direct resume, without turning that SHOULD into a universal distinct-grant MUST. Management requires its distinct/elevated resume grant at dry run and again at execute, before any member changes; the bulk token is no substitute for current authorization. Record the principal authorizing the scheduled resume with the committed schedule. Authorization is decided at scheduling, with no new fire-time grant revalidation requirement: subsequent grant expiry or withdrawal alone does not cancel an armed schedule. The timer applies only the current schedule and legal current state; it cannot revive a terminal Mission or fire a superseded schedule. On an already-suspended Mission, omitting both suspend_until and on_expiry preserves the recorded pair and its authorizing principal; supplying a valid pair replaces it subject to authorization. Existing paired-field validation remains; omission is no clearing operation and no new clear API is added. on_expiry=revoke keeps existing suspend authorization. Implement in Status and Management, including the metadata-update and bulk paths, with Document History entries, separate todo ledger rows for new observable requirements, Status re-pin and Management's first ledger pin, generated counts and both builds. | D296 | #1079 ruled (2026-10-04): ceiling drawdown cannot replenish counted derivations; option (a) with narrowing | On the owner's request for the #1079 ruling. A ceiling_drawdown successor carries forward the predecessor's committed derivation count and finite effective derivation_limit as a ceiling; a stricter applicable AS-policy or requested ceiling may narrow it, never raise or erase it. Omission cannot remove an inherited finite cap; an uncapped predecessor supplies no finite cap to inherit, and existing AS/request policy still applies. Transfer the count from the authoritative predecessor at the atomic activation/supersession boundary, serialized with predecessor issuance and duplicate creation. The synchronous successor access-token issuance consumes one further derivation in that transaction, not a second charge to the predecessor; exhaustion refuses under Derivation Limits without creating a successor, superseding the predecessor or incrementing the count. Exact recovery reuses the committed creation accounting; a fresh delivery-token issuance follows ordinary counting. Preserve D172's carryover rule, async-family and cross-domain exclusions; Template dispatch and genuinely fresh human-approved Expansion keep their existing fresh-limit rules. No per-chain wire member or aggregate/descendant budget claim. Update Derivation Limits and Progressive, conditional dependencies, history, pins and role/capability-scoped todo rows for inheritance, narrowing, exhaustion, concurrent issuance and retry; no tested claim until public-surface mutation-proved implementation witnesses exist. | D326 | #1118 ruled: establish each instance Subject and inherited authority source (2026-10-06) | Accept the proposed inheritance model with explicit bounds. A policy_drawdown child has exactly the parent Subject; a directly human-approved child defaults to that Subject and may name another only through the full authenticated Subject establishment, approval-for-that-Subject and source-authorization checks, while retaining all parent authority bounds. Ordinary children inherit the parent authority_source, including an organizational policy reference; copying provenance does not establish authority: the issuer checks that the source is applicable to the actual Subject and that the child Authority Set is within both source and parent ceilings. Preserve the separately defined, explicitly rendered carryover replacement rules. An Expansion successor, human-approved or ceiling_drawdown, keeps the predecessor Subject and authority_source; changing either needs a separately approved Mission outside this succession path. A Template instance inherits the source established at consent for the exact template version; the source must be tied to retained authenticated consent and its committed template or activation-policy state, not Dispatcher input or a silently substituted policy. Its selected Subject still follows allowed_recipients and must be authorized under that source. All realizations check the applicable source ceiling and effective expiry at their creation commit; inheritance never licenses a stale source ceiling. #1126 already repaired MAS/UMA/GNAP step lists; audit those pointers instead of repeating that work. No new public Subject selector, new binding or live-revalidation mechanism is authorized. #1069 remains the separate render-to-decision source-change implementation question. Companion specification PR authorized; ledger rows for changed observable duties, honest coverage, histories, pins and builds required. Move decide to now/P2. |

The proof covers the set, not the delta: an empty or stale `discharged` delta stays a loader obligation. | D342 | #1080 retrieval ruled (2026-10-07): a retained-result release consumes a carried single-use permit | On the owner's request to settle the retrieval question in the #1136 re-review at `24655a70`. The keyed-admission fix satisfies D333's earlier wording, but one fresh `use_limit: 1` permit can still retrieve a retained result twice and then admit a schedule under another key, reproduced independently. Adopt the stricter reference behavior: retrieval is a use of that permit. After the permit-use checks and before releasing a retained result or its retained-record disposition, atomically consume the identifier in the same durable store used by admission. Apply this to both the initial lookup and the concurrent-existing fallback. A consumed identifier refuses `permit_consumed` on retrieval or admission under any key; an unavailable store releases nothing. A later retrieval uses a fresh valid decision; retrieval still admits no operation and repeats no business effect. Permits without a use limit keep their existing key-control behavior. This extends D333's acceptance bar for the reference without adding a wire member or changing the family's idempotency contract. Tests cover two retrievals under one identifier, retrieval followed by admission, concurrent retrieval, a fresh decision retrieving the original result, and storage failure with no disclosure. The direction of local `7fde3d1a` is accepted; that commit is not approved without its final integrated-head review. #1136 also needs the stale control-options ledger note and moved documentation citations corrected before approval. No merge instruction. |

| D357 | #1153 dispatch handoff ruled (2026-10-08): a Template-specified dispatch-to-recipient handoff (#1158); #1153 stays on hold; upstream alignment widened (#1157) | On the owner's ruling after the re-review of #1153 at `15761fd6` (P2: AAM step 3, where the Dispatcher holds the instance token but the instance's approved agent is the selected Agent). Option 1, relaxing the async adapter's base-token check to the Mission Record's `client_id`, is refused: async delegation -05 Section 4.3 requires both the base token's audience and enforcement of its sender constraint, and authenticating the Agent cannot substitute for possession of a token bound to the Dispatcher's key (the Template binds the instance token to the Dispatcher's key; the continuation draft and the adapter omit the possession proof). Option 3, the AAM template listing the Dispatcher as the agent, is declined: the Dispatcher/Agent separation is the scenario's purpose. The handoff is specified in the Template profile, preferably on an existing token-exchange surface, to this bar: (1) the Dispatcher proves possession of the instance token's bound key; (2) the recipient is the instance's selected Agent, authenticated and bound to its own key; (3) the handoff preserves the Mission and cannot exceed the presented token's authority or the current effective authority; (4) the subsequent continuation satisfies upstream audience and sender-constraint requirements; (5) tests reject the wrong recipient, wrong key, wrong audience and broader authority, including recovery paths. Self-Exchange rule 1 and #1153's presented-token and recovery bounds stay (D353); rule 1's AAM consequence is #1158. The upstream-alignment issue covers both audience validation and sender-constraint enforcement, a conflict in the companion prose as well as the adapter (#1157). |
| D358 | #1158 design accepted and #1157's audience rule ruled (2026-10-08) | On the owner's "go" to the recommended answers, read as accepting both. (1) #1158: the dispatch handoff takes the child-grant shape on existing surfaces: the Dispatcher runs an RFC 8693 token exchange with the instance token and a DPoP proof of its bound key (requested_token_type `urn:ietf:params:oauth:token-type:jwt`, a Template-defined grant `typ`, no new grant type or token-type URN); the AS mints a short-lived, single-use grant naming the instance's selected Agent as its only redeemer, within the presented token's and the current effective authority; the Agent redeems it under RFC 7523 with its own authentication and key and receives an instance token issued to itself. (2) #1157, recommended option 1: the async-delegation base token is a delegation handle in draft-zhu-oauth-async-delegation-05's sense, audienced to the acting client and sender-constrained to it, with possession of its key proved at the exchange; the handoff mints such a handle for the selected Agent. #1157's adapter and continuation-draft change is its own PR. |
| D359 | #705 open question 2 ruled (2026-10-08): the recommended per-surface mapping accepted with clarifications; the coordinated migration PR may proceed | On the owner's ruling after checking main `db108910`, relayed in session. Accepted as recommended: `termination` beside `state` on every state-carrying surface; `successor` and `carried_to` move into the `superseded` and `parent_terminated` details; free-text `reason` stays separate from `termination.reason`; Discharge's `current_version` becomes `new_version` in the Discharge Result; the committed and signed `approver` names (the v1 approval-context manifest, Mandate's claim) stay, sourced from `approval_basis.consent_principal`, with no new manifest `typ` and a digest-equivalence regression witness. Clarifications the migration PR must carry: (1) A legacy incomplete termination is an explicit schema exception: a new termination requires every reason-specific field; a pre-migration termination exposes only the facts retained, never invented timestamps or references; readers still stop governed work but follow no missing successor and infer no cause-specific action; historical signatures verify before normalization, over retained original bytes. (2) Both idempotencies hold: a repeated lifecycle operation matches on state and terminal reason (`revoke` against `terminated`/`completed` stays a conflict); nonce-based response replay and request-mismatch rejection are unchanged; Management may still report `already_terminal` because the bulk objective is met, never as a successful revocation and never changing the recorded cause. (3) `parent_terminated`'s required reference is the immediate parent, consistent with the child's lineage; the triggering ancestor and propagated root cause are separate optional provenance; the child's own expiry precedence holds on ordinary cascade and Carryover alike. (4) Observed state stays separate from persistence: the once-only atomic `termination` write never delays computed expiry or the ancestor-termination projection, which work before materialization and when persistence fails; materialization preserves the established cause and references. (5) AAuth alignment stays partial: its upstream fields are unchanged; Architecture keeps that OAuth Status supports `suspended` and AAuth does not; the mapping table handles `administrative`, `parent_terminated` and unknown reasons without inventing semantic equivalence. Mandate binds `termination` to `state_at_issuance` at `iat`, so it never reads as current status. Settles open question 2's design direction and permits the migration PR; not publication approval; #705 and open question 1 stay open. |

| D366 | #1163 review (2026-10-09, at `353fe75d`): held for DPoP freshness and retryable redemption; the empty selector stated in the Template (RFC 6749 Section 3.2); the shipped `refresh_token` accepted; the restart guarantee scoped to per-boot keys; #1153 stays held | On the owner's review of #1163. Two findings, reproduced over HTTP. P1: both handoff legs accepted a DPoP proof without `iat` or a day old; RFC 9449 Section 4.3's required claims and freshness MUST be enforced before a grant is minted or consumed, a previously unseen `jti` alone not establishing freshness, with negative witnesses on both legs. P2: an authority-source outage consumed the handoff grant (503, then "already redeemed"); source availability MUST be checked before consumption and a failed issuance MUST leave the grant retryable, keeping atomic protection against concurrent redemption. Decisions: an empty `mission_dispatch_handoff` is treated as omitted, and the Template states that exception citing RFC 6749 Section 3.2; adding `refresh_token` to the shipped `subagent-invoice-extractor` is justified by the approved family-refresh path; per-boot signing keys support the restart guarantee for this deployment, and persistent keys require persistent consumption records; the resource-audienced intermediate token stays accepted under D361, and #1153 stays held for #1157 and the combined witnesses. Fixed on the branch at `60232650` (awaiting re-review): one token-endpoint proof check at both legs, also reaching child creation and expansion through `verifySubjectPossession`; the source resolved before the grant is taken; two-phase taking (reserve, confirm, release); the derivation reserved under the grant's `jti`. | D367 | #445 A20 becomes an ask and A27 is banked (2026-10-09): owning-agent withdrawal and supersession by proposal; nothing filed upstream | On the owner's "bank both", after a review of whether AAuth needs an agent-proposed, deferred lifecycle action. Ruling: no general mechanism. Changes that add authority are already proposals the person decides (-11 Section 8: proposal, `update` and `completion`, each deferrable, with AAuth Supervision as the administrative leg); AAuth has no reversible state to resume; changes that remove authority should not wait. Two narrow asks are banked, re-verified against -11 and the editor's copy (`70d67375`; the protocol draft last changed at `afeaa335`). A20, banked as a question by D148, becomes an ask: a `mission_endpoint` action that terminates the agent's own mission with reason `revoked` at once, never deferred. Section 8.6 names the owning agent among the parties that withdraw a mission, yet the agent's surface has no terminating operation and Section 8.6 places terminating at the control plane, for parties other than the owning agent. A27 asks for a proposal member naming the agent's own active mission that the new one supersedes, terminated `superseded` when the new mission is approved: Section 8.4 names no actor for that termination, a Section 8.1 proposal has no member naming a predecessor, and `expires_at` is fixed in the immutable blob (Section 8.2), so extending a mission's lifetime is a supersession. Not added: suspend or resume in AAuth, or any agent-reachable proposal channel on the control plane. The family drafts are unchanged: Management keeps the Owning Agent caller class conditional and defines no `mission_endpoint` action. `notes/aauth-engagement-package.md` still carries A20's question form and lacks A27; it is refreshed before any dispatch. Related upstream: dickhardt/AAuth#238 (revocation cascade per termination reason, another author's issue). |

An auth token's actual `iss` remains the credential issuer, distinct from the Mission Controller in four-party access. Projection happens only after native validation and grants no authority or new conformance claim. Reconcile Runtime's blanket external-join description with AAuth's existing conditional Credential-Bound claim, and clarify AuthZEN's generic reference terms and the corresponding Runtime Evidence terminology without changing their schemas or supplying Structured Authority. Add a four-party projection example, carrier/namespace/missing-input/update-stability acceptance cases, conditional role-correct ledger rows with honest coverage, histories, pins and builds. | D369 | #1154 ruled (2026-10-09): option 1, `invalid_request` for inactive-Mission gating on Token Exchange; other grants keep `invalid_grant` | On the owner's ruling, relayed in session. Bounds: (1) Token Exchange: `invalid_request` for inactive-Mission gating, including refusal after acknowledged revocation. (2) Authorization-code exchange, refresh, and JWT-bearer redemption keep `invalid_grant`. (4) `mission_error`, its disclosure restrictions, and existing companion denial reasons are preserved. Defaults adopted (not separately asked; flag if wrong):

- pnpm workspace monorepo under `src/`; TypeScript everywhere; Node 22+.
- OpenFGA runs via docker compose in its in-memory storage mode; our PDP fronts it.
- Freshness per D29/D33: the Mission Status List backs the core-tier polled
  plane; introspection (mission projection) is the immediate check on
  irreversible actions; permit-within-bound covers external commitment; the
  per-mission signed Status operation remains the authoritative surface.
  Signals push is a stretch goal.
- All state is in-memory and reseeded on boot. Record-shaped stores use
  SQLite `:memory:` behind repository interfaces (decision D27); pure
  in-process structures elsewhere; OpenFGA memory storage.
- Web apps: React + Vite SPAs.

## 3. Architecture

Operationally the stack follows the handbook's control-plane reading: the
Mission AS is the control plane for delegated authority, holding desired
state (the approved task, its authority, its lifecycle); tokens, PEPs, and
the PDP are the data plane acting within it.

```
                 +-----------------+       +-----------------+
                 |  Approver App   |       |  Operator App   |
                 |  (React SPA)    |       |  (React SPA)    |
                 +---+--------+----+       +----+------------+
                     |        |                 |
        approvals,   |        | ARAP tasks      | fleet, status,
        consent UI   |        |                 | evidence timeline
                     v        v                 v
+----------+    +---------------+    +---------------+    +----------------+
|  Agent   |    |  Mission AS   |    |     ARS       |    |      PDP       |
| Console  |    | node-oidc-    |<-->| ARAP Access   |<-->| AuthZEN API    |
| (SPA) +  |    | provider +    |    | Request Svc   |    | + contextual   |
| scenario |    | mission layer |    +---------------+    | policy view    |
| runner / |    +-------+-------+                         +-------+--------+
| LLM loop |            ^                                         |
+----+-----+            | catalog, PAR intent, code+DPoP,          v
     |                  | token exchange, DTR, txn-authz    +-------------+
     v                  |                                   |   OpenFGA   |
+---------+   MCP tools |  401 + signed txn challenge       | (in-memory) |
|  Agent  +-------------+--------------------------+        +-------------+
| (openid-|                                        |
| client, |    +-----------------------------------v-+
|  DPoP)  +--->|  MCP Payments Server (RS + PEP)      |
+---------+    |  tools, token validation, per-action |
               |  PDP calls, permits/leases, evidence |
               +--------------------------------------+
```

Not shown above: the **Transparency Service** every trusted-base component
registers evidence with; the telemetry plane (OTel collector view in Jaeger);
and the **SaaS trust domain** (RAS + LedgerCloud MCP server) the agent reaches
by redeeming a Mission-AS-issued ID-JAG at the RAS.

Trusted-base components and their spec roles:

- **Mission AS** (`services/authorization-server`): Mission Issuer. Owns intent intake
  (PAR), derivation to `mission_resource_access` authorization_details, the approval
  event, Mission Records with integrity anchors, mission-bound token issuance (DPoP),
  the subset rule, state-gated issuance/refresh, revocation by `mission_id`,
  introspection with the `mission` member, the signed Status endpoint (and
  the Mission Status List token, republished on each lifecycle transition
  per D33), the DTR
  deferred grant, and the `transaction_authorization_endpoint`. Also hosts the
  Catalog Provider role of `svc-connectivity-disco`: the Service Catalog
  Endpoint, advertised as `service_catalog_endpoint` in its own metadata,
  serving the per-user catalog with mission-derived per-connection `status`
  and `request-access` links into the ARS. As the enterprise IdP of the
  cross-domain companion, it also issues the cross-domain grant: an RFC 8693
  token exchange mints the PoP-bound, single-use ID-JAG audienced to the RAS,
  projecting only the audience-scoped Authority Set entries. Implements the
  base actor profile at issuance (nested `act` with required `act.iss`, chain
  construction/validation, local max depth, presenter continuation/rebind,
  rejection of any `actor_token` carrying `act`, `actor_unauthorized`) and
  the agent-instance profile (Client Instance Assertion carrier validation,
  `agent_instance_id` requirements, `sub_profile` of `ai_agent
  client_instance`, `ai_agent_instance_profile_supported` metadata). The
  operator app's fleet surfaces (enumeration, per-mission lifecycle
  operations) are served per the management companion (partial; O-32).
  Structured per D30: a mission-kernel module behind a typed interface,
  with node-oidc-provider hooks and the custom HTTP routes as thin
  adapters over it.
- **PDP** (`services/pdp`): AuthZEN Access Evaluation (and bulk Evaluations) API.
  Checks mission authority by deriving **contextual tuples** from the Mission
  Record per evaluation (decision D26; stored FGA tuples hold only the durable
  domain substrate), correlated by a content-addressed `policy_view_id`;
  layers the mission overlay checks
  that FGA does not model (state freshness against the staleness bound,
  parameter binding, permit property declaration, expiry), and emits
  requestable denials with
  `context.access_request` and a PDP-signed `binding_token`. Stateless by
  design (decision D28): a pure function of its inputs; permit redemption
  and lease state live at the PEP, and the freshness cache is a soft
  optimization that never changes decision semantics. Validates the shape
  and consistency of the `context.actor` chain the PEP supplies (root vs
  `subject`, leaf vs `client_instance_id`) via `packages/actor-chain`; the
  flattening itself is PEP-side (decision D31).
- **ARS** (`services/access-request`): ARAP Access Request Service. Verifies
  `binding_token` denial bindings, runs the approval task lifecycle, exposes the
  adjudication queue the approver app consumes.
- **MCP Payments Server** (`services/mcp-payments`): the resource server and PEP.
  Streamable-HTTP MCP server exposing the AP tools; validates DPoP-bound access
  tokens and the `mission` claim; constructs the AuthZEN envelope, including
  flattening the token's nested `act` chain into the root-to-leaf
  `context.actor.act` array via `packages/actor-chain` (decision D31);
  obtains a PDP permit for every consequential
  action with parameter binding and capability source context; runs the
  transaction-assurance tier for the wire transfer (single-use permit, execution
  lease, Execution Evidence, outcome reconciliation), owning permit
  redemption, lease state, and the irreversible-operation state machine per
  D28/D36 (atomic redeem-on-execute; replay refused as `permit_consumed`;
  connector idempotency keys; per-boot instance epoch bound into permits per
  D39). Authoritative for business state per D34: loads invoice/vendor
  records, builds effective parameters carrying resource versions for the
  PDP, and conditionally re-verifies them at commit; signs Transaction
  Authorization Challenges; publishes its MCP Server Card (the catalog
  references it via `server_card_uri`) and RFC 9728 protected resource
  metadata with `mission_bound_authorization_required`, plus its Enforcement
  Scope Statement. Validates proof of possession against the top-level `cnf`
  only (decision D21) and applies per-instance controls keyed on
  `(act.iss, act.sub)`. Returns a mission-scoped `tools/list` (least exposure
  at the tool boundary: the agent only sees tools within the mission's
  authority).
- **RAS + SaaS MCP Server** (`services/ras`, `services/mcp-saas`): the SaaS
  trust domain, "LedgerCloud" (accounting SaaS). The RAS is a second
  node-oidc-provider instance whose custom RFC 7523 JWT-bearer grant redeems
  ID-JAGs per the cross-domain companion: signature against the Mission AS
  JWKS, audience, proof-of-possession, and a single-use replay check, then
  mints short-lived local access tokens preserving `mission.id`,
  `mission.issuer`, and `authority_hash`. The SaaS MCP server (vendor bank
  details, journal entry posting) declares the MCP EMA extension in its
  authorization metadata and enforces from the token alone (including a
  `tools/list` filtered to the token's granted authority): the estate runs
  at lifetime-bounded reliance, in deliberate contrast with the
  Runtime-Enforced internal estate.
- **Transparency Service** (`services/transparency`): the audit draft's SCITT
  Transparency Service, in memory: append-only Merkle log, COSE Signed
  Statements committed by hash envelope, Receipts and signed tree heads,
  per-mission feeds (`sub` is the Mission). The log holds commitments only;
  producers retain their own evidence and Receipts (decision D32). The AS,
  PDP, and MCP server register their evidence; a CLI verifier and the
  operator app run the draft's five-step verification, and from M11 the
  operator timeline's normal read path is the verified feed itself.
- **Agent** (`services/agent`): OAuth client built on panva `openid-client`
  (PAR + DPoP + token exchange), MCP client, scripted scenario runner, optional
  LLM loop. Declares the MCP EMA extension capability
  (`io.modelcontextprotocol/enterprise-managed-authorization`) at `initialize`
  and drives the ID-JAG acquisition/redemption for the SaaS domain. Each
  agent instance holds a per-instance key and presents Client Instance
  Assertion evidence (`agent_instance_id`, `agent_platform`, `agent_model`);
  sub-agent spawn is a further token exchange presenting the sub-agent's own
  evidence as the `actor_token`. Hosts the shaper module (intent proposals
  are untrusted input per the shaping draft) and the minimal harness duty:
  on resume it checks mission Status and stops on a non-active state before
  attempting any action. Acts as the Agent Console's BFF and exclusively
  owns the OAuth, DPoP, and client keys (decision D35).
- **Console BFF** (`services/console-bff`): owns approver/operator browser
  sessions (HttpOnly SameSite cookies), fronts the AS management surfaces
  and the ARS queue, and hosts the feed-driven audit read model behind the
  operator timeline: joins across producers, orphan detection, and
  sequence-gap checks per `(Mission, emitter)` (decisions D32/D35).
- **Apps** (`apps/approver`, `apps/operator`, `apps/agent-console`): persona UIs.
  Browsers never hold service credentials.

Cross-cutting: every service adopts `packages/telemetry` (OpenTelemetry with
W3C trace context propagated across OAuth requests, PDP evaluations, MCP tool
calls, and evidence registrations, exported to Jaeger; pino structured logs
carrying `trace_id` and `mission_id`). Evidence objects carry the producing
span's `trace_id` as an extension member (decision D13).

Two demo-correctness facilities: (1) a **headless adjudication path**, a
test-only API to approve or deny as a seeded approver, drives approvals in
scenario and eval runs; it is clearly marked test-only, disabled outside
dev, and its use is visible in the evidence so evals cannot be fooled by
it. (2) The **side-effect oracle**: the payments ledger, the email outbox,
and the SaaS journal record every mutation together with the authorizing
permit or token identity, giving evals ground truth for "zero unauthorized
side effects".

### Demo domain model (accounts payable)

Seeded entities: principals `alice` (mission owner) and `bob` (approver / AP manager);
OAuth client `ap-agent` (DPoP-bound); vendors `acme` (approved) and `globex`
(not yet approved); invoices in several amounts, at least one above the mission's
payment cap; a payments ledger for reconciliation. The catalog seeds three
services: the internal payments MCP server, the LedgerCloud SaaS MCP server
(an `id_jag` connection naming the RAS as `authorization_server`), and an
out-of-reach `hr-files` service (no authority path for `alice`) whose entry
carries a `request-access` link into the ARS.

Tools on the MCP server (action classification per runtime § action classification floor):

| Tool | Class | Enforcement tier |
|---|---|---|
| `list_invoices`, `get_invoice`, `lookup_vendor` | read | core tier |
| `schedule_payment` | consequential, reversible | core tier |
| `execute_wire_transfer` | high-consequence, irreversible | transaction-assurance tier |
| `send_remittance_email` | external communication | transaction-assurance tier |
| `get_vendor_bank_details` (SaaS) | read | token-only (lifetime-bounded estate) |
| `post_journal_entry` (SaaS) | consequential, reversible | token-only (lifetime-bounded estate) |

### OpenFGA model sketch

Types: `user`, `client`, `mission`, `vendor`, `invoice`, `payment_batch`.
Stored tuples hold only the durable domain substrate (invoice ownership,
vendor approval state, roles such as Bob's AP manager). Mission authority is
injected per check as **contextual tuples** derived from the Mission
Record's `authority_set` (for example `mission:m1#payer@invoice:inv-42`),
computed by the PDP alongside the state/freshness check it already performs
(decision D26). There is no tuple writer keyed to mission lifecycle and no
dual-write: revocation and completion take effect through the record itself
(state check precedes FGA). `policy_view_id` is the content hash of the
Mission Record version plus the FGA model version. Numeric constraints
(per-payment cap, cumulative caps) are evaluated with FGA conditions where
they fit and in the PDP overlay where they do not; see issue O-6.

### End-to-end scenarios (the demo script)

0. **Discovery bootstrap**: agent signs in, reads `service_catalog_endpoint`
   from the AS metadata, makes a scoped catalog request (`type=mcp` plus a
   category/tag filter), selects the payments server, re-anchors trust via the
   server's protected resource metadata, and reads its Server Card before
   shaping intent. With no mission yet, the connection reports
   `consent_required`.
1. **Issuance**: the shaper proposes intent (untrusted input per the shaping
   draft), the agent submits it via PAR, the issuer derives the authority,
   and Bob, the AP manager, approves in the approver app (intent + authority
   set + anchors rendered): the mission carries writes, so subject and
   approver must differ per D37, with Alice as the mission's subject.
   Mission-bound DPoP token issued; operator app shows the new Mission.
   Includes the compromised-shaper test: an over-broad proposal never widens
   the derived authority.
2. **Happy path**: agent pays an in-authority invoice under the cap; per-action
   PDP permits; Decision + Execution Evidence visible in the operator timeline.
3. **Parameter binding / TOCTOU**: scenario mutates payment params between
   decision and execution; digest mismatch, PEP refuses, Refusal Record logged.
4. **Wire transfer**: transaction-assurance tier end to end: single-use permit,
   execution lease, execution, outcome reconciliation against the ledger.
5. **ARAP reevaluate**: `action_approval_required` denial at the PEP; access
   request to ARS; Bob adjudicates; PEP re-evaluates with `context.approval`;
   action proceeds. No new token issued.
6. **AROP / DTR**: agent requests authority for vendor `globex` at the token
   endpoint with `completion_mode=deferred`; requestable denial; `deferral_code`;
   Bob approves; AS records a Mission Expansion (successor mission) and issues
   the token carrying the successor's `mission` claim; PDP view updates.
7. **AROP / Transaction Challenge**: MCP server returns 401 with a signed
   `transaction_challenge` for an over-cap wire; agent presents it at the AS
   `transaction_authorization_endpoint`; approval; txn-bound audience-restricted
   single-use token; re-presented and honored exactly once.
8. **Revocation freshness**: operator revokes mid-mission; a wire transfer
   is denied instantly (immediate-check branch), while a read is denied
   within the published staleness bound (polled-cache branch); issuance and
   refresh are also gated. The two-tier contrast is the demo.
9. **Completion**: mission completes; residual tokens no longer authorize.
10. **Catalog reflection**: per-connection `status` tracks the fleet
    (`connected` while the mission is active, `unavailable` after revocation),
    and the out-of-reach service's `request-access` link opens an ARS intake
    that, once adjudicated, flips its status.
11. **Transparent audit**: evidence from the scenarios above is registered as
    Signed Statements; the operator assembles the mission's feed, runs the
    five-step offline verification, and follows a Decision Evidence record's
    `trace_id` into Jaeger; tampering with stored evidence or dropping a
    registered record is detected.
12. **Cross-domain via EMA/ID-JAG**: the catalog lists LedgerCloud with an
    `id_jag` connection; the agent declares the EMA extension at `initialize`,
    obtains a PoP-bound single-use ID-JAG from the Mission AS by token
    exchange (audience-scoped authority only), redeems it at the RAS for a
    short-lived local token preserving the mission anchors, and posts the
    journal entry for the executed wire. A replayed grant is rejected; after
    mission revocation the next grant request is refused at the issuer, and
    the residual local token dies with its lease (lifetime-bounded estate).
13. **Sub-agent delegation**: the orchestrator agent spawns a sub-agent for
    invoice triage; the token exchange presents the sub-agent's instance
    assertion as the `actor_token`, authority narrows to a subset, the
    token's nested `act` grows a hop while `sub` is preserved, the PDP
    evaluates the root-to-leaf chain, and revoking the sub-agent's instance
    at the PEP kills only the sub-agent (the orchestrator continues).
14. **The 02:00 resume**: while the agent idles, the mission completes (or is
    cancelled); on wake the agent's harness check reads Status, sees the
    non-active state, and stops before attempting any action; if the check
    is bypassed, the PEP denies and the Refusal Record is written (the
    handbook's running example, both fears closed).

### The five laws, enforced

The handbook's five laws map onto the build as follows; the M14
self-assessment walks this table.

| Law | Enforced by | Scenarios |
|---|---|---|
| Durability | the Mission Record outlives sessions and tokens; signed Status is the authoritative state | 1, 8, 9, 14 |
| Attribution | subject/approver on the record; `act` chains + instance identity; evidence that joins | 1, 11, 13 |
| Narrowing | subset rule, audience-scoped projection, caps, delegation narrows | 6, 7, 12, 13 |
| Termination | state-gated issuance, revocation, completion, freshness bounds, cross-domain lease expiry | 8, 9, 12, 14 |
| Containment | per-action PEP/PDP, parameter binding, single-use permits, per-instance revocation | 2, 3, 4, 13 |

## 4. Repo Layout

```
src/
  PLAN.md                     this document
  package.json                workspace root (scripts: dev, seed, test, demo)
  pnpm-workspace.yaml
  docker-compose.yml          OpenFGA (in-memory storage mode) + Jaeger
  .env.example                ports, issuer URL, optional ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
  packages/
    mission-core/             shared types (Mission Record, mission claim, AuthZEN
                              envelope, evidence objects), canonicalization +
                              intent_hash / authority_hash, core test vectors
    authzen-client/           PEP-side AuthZEN client (evaluation, bulk, retries)
    demo-data/                seed loaders: users, clients, vendors, invoices,
                              FGA store + model + tuples
    store/                    SQLite :memory: repositories (missions, approvals,
                              tasks, permits, oracles, catalog, evidence index)
    telemetry/                shared OTel + pino setup (trace context, ids)
    actor-chain/              act-chain validation + nested-to-root-to-leaf
                              flattening, shared by AS, PDP, and PEPs
  services/
    authorization-server/     mission-kernel module + thin adapters
                              (node-oidc-provider hooks, custom routes)
    pdp/                      AuthZEN PDP + OpenFGA integration
    access-request/           ARAP ARS
    mcp-payments/             MCP server + RS/PEP + payments API + ledger
    ras/                      SaaS-domain Resource AS (node-oidc-provider,
                              JWT-bearer ID-JAG redemption)
    mcp-saas/                 LedgerCloud SaaS MCP server (token-only
                              enforcement, EMA declared)
    transparency/             SCITT Transparency Service (audit draft)
    console-bff/              approver/operator sessions + audit read model
    agent/                    OAuth+MCP client, scenario runner, LLM loop
  apps/
    approver/                 approvals inbox (missions, ARAP tasks, deferred queue)
    operator/                 fleet dashboard, evidence timeline, status controls
    agent-console/            chat + scenario runner UI, live token/mission state
  evals/
    suites/                   adversarial + legitimate-flow eval cases
    runner/                   drives agent modes, scores runs, emits scorecards
```

Port map (defaults, overridable via `.env`): AS 4400, PDP 4401, ARS 4402,
MCP/payments 4403, transparency 4404, RAS 4405, SaaS MCP 4406, console-bff
4407, approver 5173, operator 5174, agent-console 5175, OpenFGA 8080 (http,
pre-shared auth + TLS) / 8081 (grpc), playground disabled, Jaeger 16686
(UI) / 4317 (OTLP).

### Testing and delivery

- Traceability: each scenario (0-14) is one named spec file with a stable
  ID, referenced from milestone exits (D40).
- Tests: vitest across the workspace. Unit tests per package (anchor vectors
  in `mission-core`); scenario tests compose all services **in-process**
  (everything is in-memory, so one Node process can host the full stack),
  with real HTTP reserved for wire-shape assertions (PAR, token endpoint,
  AuthZEN, MCP transport); golden files for PDP decisions and exhibits.
- CI: a dedicated GitHub Actions workflow gated on `src/**` path filters so
  the draft-build/publish CI and the implementation CI never interfere.
  Jobs: lint, typecheck, unit, scenario integration (OpenFGA service
  container), evals (from M13).
- Toolchain: Node and pnpm pinned via `engines` + corepack; lockfile
  committed. Dependency policy per D39: `oidc-provider` pinned to the first
  version that passes the pre-flight spike; the OpenFGA image pinned by
  digest; the MCP SDK and spec revision pinned (D38: stable 2025-11-25
  authorization profile).
- Spec traceability (D41): `src/SPEC_VERSIONS.md` is a matrix mapping every
  implemented spec to its pinned version, its implementing components, and
  its conformance tests; spec-derived code carries greppable
  `@spec <doc>#<section>` tags. A spec update becomes a change list by
  diffing the spec from its pinned version and following the touched
  sections through the matrix and tags. Goal 2 means companions will change
  during implementation; version bumps are deliberate, reviewed against the
  Spec Feedback Log, never implicit, and each bump commit names the
  affected modules and tests.

Definition of done, every milestone: tests green, the spec-feedback pass
done, this status table and the logs updated, the PR merged.

Each milestone lands as its own PR with tests; acceptance criteria are the exit
bar. Every milestone's exit also includes a **spec-feedback pass**: anything
found during the milestone that is ambiguous, disproportionately hard to
implement, over-complex, or non-interoperable lands in the Spec Feedback Log
(§ 8) with a category and disposition before the milestone closes.

Before M1 design hardens, a **pre-flight spike** burns down the pin-type
issues gating the early milestones: O-1 (PAR intent carriage), O-25
(CIA-CORE carriers), O-26 (entity-profile values), and O-27 (chain
depth/rebind) by reading; and O-2 (node-oidc-provider fit) as a timeboxed
coding spike with an explicit go/fallback decision (fallback: thin custom
endpoints beside the provider). Per D30, the go/fallback decision is scoped
to the AS's adapter layer only; the mission-kernel is unaffected either
way. Results land in the issue log and, where they expose spec friction,
in the Spec Feedback Log.

- **M0. Scaffolding.** Workspace, tsconfig, lint, docker-compose (OpenFGA +
  Jaeger), `packages/telemetry` (the OTel + pino baseline every service
  adopts), `packages/store` (the SQLite `:memory:` repository baseline,
  decision D27), `mission-core` with canonicalization + anchors passing the
  core test vectors (`draft-mcguinness-oauth-mission` § test vectors), and
  four architecture artifacts: `payments-runtime-profile-v1` (the Operation
  Profile, D34), the channel/auth/key matrix with the trusted-base
  statement (D39/D40), the approval and irreversible-operation state
  machines (D36/D37), and the FGA hygiene policy (D39). Also lands
  `src/LICENSE` (BSD-2-Clause) and the `license` fields (D40).
  *Exit: `pnpm test` green on anchor vectors; `docker compose up` serves
  OpenFGA (pre-shared auth + TLS) and Jaeger; a sample service's span is
  visible in Jaeger; the four artifacts reviewed and committed.*
- **M1. Baseline Issuance AS.** PAR intent intake, derivation, approval event
  (minimal approver page), Mission Record store, `mission` claim + DPoP binding,
  subset rule, state-gated issuance/refresh, revocation by `mission_id`,
  introspection `mission` member, signed Status endpoint, AS metadata flags.
  *Exit: core conformance checklist items 1-6 (core § Conformance) demonstrably met;
  scenario 1 runs headless, including the compromised-shaper test; a thin
  tracer slice (PAR intent -> approval -> token -> minimal PDP evaluation ->
  `get_invoice` through an MCP skeleton, wire-real but throwaway-grade) runs
  end to end before M2 begins, surfacing URI/token/evidence mismatches
  early.*
- **M2. Actor profile + agent instance.** Base actor-profile conformance at
  the AS (chain construction/validation, presenter transitions, local max
  depth, errors, metadata, introspection) with `packages/actor-chain` shared
  by AS, PDP, and PEPs; full ai-agent-instance profile (per-instance keys,
  Client Instance Assertion carrier validation, instance claims, metadata
  flags). Foundational: the PDP envelope, PEP controls, and agent identity
  in every later milestone consume these surfaces.
  *Exit: delegated issuance produces conformant chains in token-level
  integration tests, including rejection of an `actor_token` that itself
  carries `act` and the actor-chain flattening vectors; the end-to-end
  sub-agent demo (scenario 13) lands with M12.*
- **M3. PDP + OpenFGA.** AuthZEN evaluation + evaluations endpoints, envelope
  parsing (note: approved-entry `resource` matches `context.audience`, not the
  AuthZEN `resource` member), `context.actor` shape/consistency validation
  via `packages/actor-chain` (flattening is PEP-side per D31),
  FGA model for the domain substrate with mission authority
  injected as contextual tuples derived from the Mission Record per check
  (decision D26), content-addressed `policy_view_id`, freshness per D33
  (Mission Status List fetch per window backs the core-tier polled plane;
  introspection with the mission projection is the immediate check for the
  irreversible class).
  *Exit: golden-file decision tests: in-authority allow, out-of-authority deny,
  revoked-mission deny within bound.*
- **M4. MCP server + core enforcement tier.** AP tools, streamable HTTP, RFC 9728
  PRM, token + `mission` claim validation, per-action PDP calls with
  `context.mission` / `context.actor` / `context.audience` / `parameter_digest`,
  mission-scoped `tools/list` filtering (least exposure),
  per-instance controls keyed on `(act.iss, act.sub)`, Decision Evidence and
  Refusal Records, Enforcement Scope Statement published.
  *Exit: scenarios 2 and 3 pass as integration tests.*
- **M5. Transaction-assurance tier.** Single-use permits and execution
  leases (properties declared by the PDP in the decision; redemption and
  lease state owned by the PEP per D28), Execution Evidence, outcome
  reconciliation for `execute_wire_transfer` and `send_remittance_email`.
  *Exit: scenario 4; a replayed permit is refused as `permit_consumed`;
  reconciliation report joins evidence to ledger entries.*
- **M6. ARAP reevaluate mode.** Requestable denials from the PDP
  (`context.access_request` + PDP-signed `binding_token`), ARS task lifecycle,
  approver adjudication UI, PEP re-evaluation with `context.approval`.
  *Exit: scenario 5; approval is provably input context (no token change).*
- **M7. AROP.** DTR custom grant (`completion_mode=deferred`, `deferral_code`,
  deferred grant polling, idempotent submission, approval-bounded lifetime) and
  Transaction Challenge (RS challenge signing + `txn_challenge_jwks_uri`, AS
  `transaction_authorization_endpoint`, txn-bound audience-restricted single-use
  token, re-presentation checks), both completing through Mission Expansion.
  *Exit: scenarios 6 and 7; issued tokens never broaden the originating request
  and never outlive `approved_until`.*
- **M8. Service connectivity discovery.** Catalog Provider co-located in the
  AS: Service Catalog Endpoint with filtering (`category`, `type`, `status`,
  `profile`, `tag`), `service_catalog_endpoint` in AS metadata, entries seeded
  from demo-data, mission-derived per-connection `status`, `request-access`
  links into the ARS, and the payments server's Server Card published and
  referenced via `server_card_uri`. The LedgerCloud `id_jag` entry is seeded
  here and becomes actionable with M9.
  *Exit: scenarios 0 and 10 pass headless; catalog status flips on approval,
  revocation, and expansion without restart.*
- **M9. Cross-domain SaaS leg (EMA + ID-JAG).** Second trust domain per the
  cross-domain companion: Mission AS token-exchange issuance of the
  cross-domain grant with audience-scoped projection; RAS (second
  node-oidc-provider) with the JWT-bearer redemption grant, PoP and
  single-use validation, mission-preserving local tokens; LedgerCloud SaaS
  MCP server with EMA declared, enforcing from the token alone; catalog
  entry with the `id_jag` connection; agent EMA capability and flow.
  *Exit: scenario 12 passes as integration tests, including grant replay
  rejection and the revocation-lease demonstration.*
- **M10. Transparent audit (SCITT).** Transparency Service per the audit
  draft: in-memory append-only Merkle log, COSE Signed Statements with
  hash-envelope commitments, Receipts and signed tree heads; registration
  hooks in the AS, PDP, and MCP server for every evidence type the draft
  fixes; per-mission feed retrieval; CLI verifier plus an operator app audit
  view running the five-step offline check; `trace_id` extension member on
  evidence.
  *Exit: scenario 11 passes headless, including the tamper demo (mutated
  evidence fails digest verification, a dropped record fails inclusion).*
- **M11 scope note.** The three SPAs (`apps/approver`, `apps/operator`,
  `apps/agent-console`) are thin views over `services/console-bff` (D35);
  interactive UI is not headlessly verifiable, so M11's testable substance is
  the BFF persona layer (sessions, role/CSRF, approver queue + adjudication,
  operator fleet + lifecycle, the D32 feed-driven evidence timeline), covered
  by `console-bff/test`. The scenarios are "runnable from the UIs" at the BFF
  API the SPAs consume; a manual UI pass is a follow-on, not a gate.

- **M11. Full UX.** The three persona apps complete: approvals inbox with intent
  rendering, fleet dashboard on the management companion's surfaces
  (enumeration, revoke/expand, status transitions), the evidence timeline
  assembled feed-first per D32 (walk the mission's transparency feed,
  retrieve from producers, verify digests on render) joining decisions,
  executions, refusals, and reconciliation, and the agent console's
  discovery/catalog view.
  *Exit: scenarios 0-12 all runnable from the UIs alone (13 and 14 join in
  M12).*
- **M12. Agent + demos.** Scenario runner covering scenarios 0-14; the
  minimal harness duty in the agent (Status check on resume, stop on
  non-active); orchestrator/sub-agent support (scenario 13); optional LLM
  chat mode; seed polish; a `pnpm demo` one-command boot; `DEMO.md`, the
  guided walkthrough matching the runner (D40); and the exhibit
  mode emitting annotated wire captures shaped like the handbook's
  Appendix B.
  *Exit: fresh clone to full demo in under five minutes; scenarios 0-14 pass
  headless via the runner, including per-instance revocation (13) and the
  02:00 resume (14).*
- **M13. Evals.** The eval harness (`evals/`): a deterministic adversarial
  suite driving misbehaving agents at the running stack (prompt-injected
  tool output steering the agent off-mission, over-broad shaper proposals,
  parameter mutation between decision and execution, out-of-authority tool
  calls, sub-agent escalation attempts, replayed permits and cross-domain
  grants, confused-deputy attempts against the RAS, resumed work on a dead
  mission) and a legitimate-flow suite (scenarios 0-14 as the baseline);
  optional LLM red-team mode (Anthropic API) generating adversarial agent
  behavior against the same scoring. Each run is scored on: containment
  (unauthorized side effects MUST be zero), denial correctness (right
  `denial_reason` for the right cause), evidence completeness (every
  consequential attempt joins decision, execution or refusal, and audit
  records), over-blocking rate on the legitimate suite, and freshness-bound
  compliance. Emits a scorecard artifact; regressions gate CI.
  *Exit: `pnpm evals` runs headless in CI with 100% containment and zero
  evidence gaps on the adversarial suite; over-blocking on the legitimate
  suite is at or below the threshold set in O-30; red-team mode produces a
  reproducible transcript + scorecard when an API key is present.*
- **M14. Conformance + reports.** The written self-assessment against the
  six Runtime-Enforced invariants, the handbook vendor test's six questions,
  and the Field Reference implementation checklist; the five-laws table
  walked with links into recorded evidence; the consolidated spec-feedback
  report drawn from the Spec Feedback Log (goal 2's deliverable).
  `pnpm demo:vendor-test` runs the four valid-token-but-denied cases back to
  back (state: scenario 8, bounds: 7, parameters: 3, delegation chain: 13).
  *Exit: all assessments published in-repo; the vendor-test demo passes on
  the eval-gated build.*

resolution and date; never delete them.

### Open

- **O-3. DTR draft fidelity.** Fetch `draft-gerber-oauth-deferred-token-response`
  and pin parameter names, error codes (`authorization_pending`, `slow_down`,
  `expired_token`), and the deferred grant URN before M7.
  Disposition (2026-07-26): pinned to `-00` (PR #350) — parameter names, the
  deferred grant URN, and the error vocabulary verified/aligned (`invalid_grant`
  routing for unknown/already-redeemed fixed).
  Resolved (2026-07-28, PR #353): the residual is closed — `slow_down` /
  `expired_token` backoff added to `deferred.ts` (RFC 8628: advertises
  `interval+5`, 600s lifetime), and the deferred grant is wired onto the real
  `/token` endpoint via `registerGrantType`. Redemption mints a resource-bound
  JWT mission token (not opaque), DPoP-bound (`cnf.jkt` from the request proof),
  TTL clamped by `approved_until`, carrying the active Mission unchanged (D42).
  Initiation is folded into the grant type (deviation S-14). SPEC_VERSIONS row
  51 updated; real-HTTP coverage in `dtr-endpoint.test.ts`.
- **O-4. Transaction challenge draft fidelity.** Fetch
  `draft-rosomakho-oauth-txn-challenge` and pin the challenge JWS claims
  (`txn`, `authorization_details`, `iss`, `aud`, `reason`), the
  `Accept-Txn-Challenge` header, endpoint request/response shapes, and
  `txn_challenge_jwks_uri` metadata before M7.
  Disposition (2026-07-26): resolved against `-00` (PR #350) — challenge JWS
  typ (`txn-authz-challenge+jwt`)/`jti`/`txn` claims, endpoint request/response,
  §6.2 token-vs-challenge binding, and poll error codes aligned. The
  `parameter_digest` gap is logged as S-10; the in-process/transport
  simplifications (401→`access_challenge`, `Accept-Txn-Challenge`, form-vs-JSON,
  `txn_challenge_jwks_uri` served) as S-11.
- **O-5. Expansion lifecycle detail.** Read the expansion draft closely: successor

- **O-6. Per-payment cap placement.** FGA condition vs PDP overlay for the

- **O-8. Freshness numbers.** The authoritative-source question is resolved

- **O-9. COAZ alignment.** mission-authzen composes with COAZ for MCP tool

- **O-10. ARAP draft fidelity.** Fetch the ARAP profile itself (access request

  Resolved (2026-07-27) against ARAP (openid/authzen PR #508, blob

  `expires_at` + `binding_token`, an ARAP mapping note; PR #351) and the impl

  ARS approval-state JWS carries `iss`+`aud`; PR #352). SPEC_VERSIONS row 48

- **O-11. Consent Evidence scope.** The approver app renders intent at approval;

- **O-12. Mission-derived status mapping.** Exact mapping from mission

- **O-15. request-access intake shape.** What the `request-access` href

- **O-16. COSE and Merkle tooling.** Pick the TS COSE_Sign1 library (or

- **O-18. trace_id extension member.** Name and placement of the trace
  correlation member on evidence objects (it is part of the signed and

- **O-19. ID-JAG draft fidelity.** Fetch
  `draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant` and pin the token

  Resolved (2026-07-27) against draft-04. Redemption is RFC 7523 JWT-bearer

- **O-20. EMA metadata surface.** Pin exactly how the SaaS MCP server

- **O-21. Catalog status for id_jag connections.** The mission-derived

- **O-22. Audience-scoped projection derivation.** How the Mission AS
  decides which Authority Set entries a given RAS is authoritative for

- **O-23. SaaS-side audit registration.** Whether the RAS registers grant
